 Labyrinth (Claudio PRC Deep mix)
 Review: UK pair Atypikal have been deep in production, sound design and audio engineering since the 90s and often explore the more experimental fringes. Fur:ther Sessions encourage just that on this new EP, which kicks off with the whirring machines and distant clangs of deep, foreboding roller 'Monolith.' The don Mike Parker emixe sit with extra thump in the kicks and pairs back the noise to a pair of synth leads - one wispy and pixel thin, the other bloated and warped. 'Labyrinth' ramps up the pressure with polyrhythmic lines and layers of water, liquid sound that pool all through the mix and serve as a wormhole to another dimension. It's an artful representation of a complete mental breakdown, but Claudio PRC's deeper rework allows you to gather yourself and enjoy a more serene cosmic trip. … Read more

 Stop Phoning Me
 Review: Glasgow techno heavyweight Gary Beck has been refining his hard-hitting style for many years, and with the title cut of this new one - the 50th EP on the Don't label - he collides two very different worlds to blistering effect. 'Glasgow Choir' is filthy dirty techno with dust, grit and distortion in every bar, but is made almost religious with some choral vocals spread through them. It's like Floorplan had he grown up on the River Clyde rather than the Motor City. Elsewhere, 'Heard Of K' is loopy and muscular, Jerome Hill adds a subtle rework and 'Stop Phoning Me' is a pure kick drum workout with more spring than a hundred mattresses. … Read more

 Mog (7:07)
 Review: Sweden's Grovskopa (who also has a fine catalogue of bold, loopy techno under his Rivet alias) keeps things lean and purposeful on Mord with four-to-the-floor pressure and still very little interest in unnecessary decoration. The appeal is in the details: clipped percussion, shifting basslines and subtle changes that gradually tighten the screws rather than going for obvious peaks. There's a neat crossover between stripped-back minimalism and tougher techno on the juggling 'Kagig' while 'Prega' endlessly falls in on itself only to rise, rinse and repeat. 'Blaga' has more industrial roughness to its metal texture and 'Mog' has an urgency that gets your back up because of the world's human voice that rips through the mix. Loop techno perfection.

 Review: Sunju Hargun, the Thailand-based producer, DJ and owner of the brilliant 'In the Fog' Substack, is the pioneer for the London event series Transmissions' brand spanking new label. Their first release is a sought after vinyl pressing of Hargun's 2021 EP 'Mooping Cuts', which was originally self-released. Fun fact: this EP was dedicated to Hargun's cat Mooping and the tracks utilise ASMR recordings of his feline friend's vocalisations. On this 2026 version, the psychedelic techno aficionado completely remasters the record whilst also bringing some timely reworks into the fold, such as 'Pigeon Attack (2026 Version)', which is a slightly juiced up revamp full of hypnotic melodies and soothing frequencies folded into trancey percussion. 'Phaya Naga' is a dubbier number with a spiritual and reverberant vocal leading you on guided techno meditation, before the title track grumbles and swells its way into the cosmos. In turn, the 2026 rework of this title track ups the ante a bit and injects some welcome oomph through its rolling percussion and quivering bassline, making this particular version club ready. After four tracks of mind-altering goodness thanks to Mooping, one can only hope he gets plenty of fishy treats for his crucial contributions. … Read more

This four tracker marks the return of Inner Lakes to New York label Earth Dog, and it's just as brilliant and devious as his debut on the label.  Review: Well well well... The inimitable Pev returns after a breif release respite, and he does so with a new concept. Following three years and five EPs of the Pulse series, he now presents a collection of Wrecks on 'Pace The Wrecks'. Quite whether it's going to build into a similar scale body of work or will just remain as a standalone, we don't know, but each track is numbered in the same way and there definitely feels like he's starting to tell a new story. Perhaps a story that's rooted in the more high energy, euphoric corners of the continuum. 'Wreck 1' conjures memories of Get Physical in its earliest forms. A very heady, unhurried state of trance. 'Wreck II' is much more upbeat and Detroitian, laden with emotion and suspence. Flip for a trip back in time with the acid house bubbles and dreams of 'Wreck III' before 'Wreck IV' brings things back home to the future with a dubby, steppy slice of cosmic two-step in a way that only he could. Wreck yourself before you check yourself. … Read more

 Review: Argentinian Translate tends towards the more abstract end of the techno spectrum. His EarToGround debut is stacked with four cuts of deep, hypnotic rhythm that hums and buzzes like a substation. 'Misdirection' soon settles into a linear odyssey with rolling kicks left unhindered as tactile synths roam about up top. 'Kw' has a little more bite in the percussion but everything is still very much locked in place as waves of voltage surge through the grooves. 'Cfg' carries on down the same path with endless loops that subtly change with each rotation as your mind melts more and more and 'Gas' then takes you deep into an interplanetary abyss with sci-fi transmissions and the sound of spacecraft circling the drums. … Read more

 Bw - "Olives"
 Review: Spain's New Rhythmic assembles another crew of forward-looking techno producers for their latest various artists EP. Gareth Wild shows a great mastery of drama on 'Boca De Venum', which has plenty of open space and an eerie lead synth rising out as a bassline roll with the force of a freight train. Joton & Divide's 'Junction' is busier, with detuned synths mending between jacked analogue drums and distorted texture. A Morgan's 'Loathing' is more streamlined but no less impactful, with howling synths and minimal drums all racing along at pace. Bw steals the show at the end for us - 'Olives' is pure mind-melting deep tech complete with bendy synths that warp space and time once you've been locked in long enough. … Read more

 Double Shot (4:35)
 Review: Venerated techno veteran Speedy J and Berlin's dark lord Rodhad unite as The Department of Almost for Second Office, a debut LP developed over ten concentrated days in Berlin's WSNWG studio. The 11 tracks find the pair fold propulsive dancefloor material into spacious, contemplative passages, favouring gradual transformation over dramatic gesture. Rather than a one-off collaboration, the album is said to follow years of creative exchange between two long-established artistic voices.  Der Letzte Raum (3:10)
 Review: Germany-born Vril has long made techno that feels like transmissions from another world, but KRUX finds the now Portugal-based producer opening the doors to new voices. The 13-track album retains his signature dub-heavy atmosphere while pushing into stranger, more unpredictable territory. Rene Wise joins for the churning pressure of 'Wave Collapse' while Aurora Halal and Verraco bring their own distinct energies to the fold. Across the record, machinery and human touch sit in uneasy balance, from shadowy hypnosis to more fractured rhythms. It's a compelling shift in direction. … Read more
